{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01NxocT69G4wQqgs5DaRE9DB","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Report usage across every tool round, not just the last\n2\t\n3\tIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#93\n4\tLabels: priority:medium, type:feature, size:S, area:api\n5\t\n6\t## Why\n7\t\n8\t`afterModelResponse` is the hook a deployer reaches for when they need to record what a turn cost — an audit entry, a billing line, a usage budget. It is called once per user message, after the round loop exits, and is handed the `lastUsage` local: `chatOrchestrator.ts` assigns `lastUsage = outcome.usage ?? lastUsage` on each round, so what the hook receives is the usage of the final provider call alone.\n9\t\n10\tFor a turn that answered directly, that is the whole turn. For a turn that called tools, it is the last leg of several, and the earlier rounds — including the one carrying the customer's question and the full tool results — are not counted anywhere. A deployer billing or budgeting from this hook under-counts exactly the expensive turns, and nothing in the signature hints at it. The current behaviour is recorded in `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md` so it is at least discoverable, but documenting a surprising number is weaker than not producing one.\n11\t\n12\tRaised by the reviewer on HALEngine#83, which recorded the behaviour but could not change it: that PR's ticket scoped itself to specifying the seam, not altering the orchestrator.\n13\t\n14\t## What to do\n15\t\n16\tDecide, and make the signature say the answer. Two shapes are worth weighing, and the issue should record why the other was rejected:\n17\t\n18\t- Accumulate across rounds and pass the sum, keeping one `usage` argument. Simple for the common case, and it silently changes what an existing consumer records — the number gets larger for tool-heavy turns, which is the point, but it is a behaviour change worth a MINOR bump and a changelog line that says so.\n19\t- Pass both, for example the summed total plus the per-round breakdown, so a consumer that wants provider-call granularity keeps it. More faithful, wider surface.\n20\t\n21\tWhichever is chosen, a turn that ran no tools must report exactly what it reports today, so the common path does not move.\n22\t\n23\t## Acceptance criteria\n24\t\n25\t- [ ] A turn driven through two tool rounds reports usage covering every provider call in that turn, asserted against a scripted provider whose rounds report known, different token counts\n26\t- [ ] A turn with no tool calls reports exactly what it reports today, pinned by the existing test remaining unedited\n27\t- [ ] A provider that reports no usage on some rounds does not make the total `undefined` or `NaN`: the rounds that reported are still counted, and a turn where nothing reported still reports nothing\n28\t- [ ] The type of what the hook receives makes the answer unambiguous, so a reader does not have to open the orchestrator to learn whether it is a sum\n29\t- [ ] `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md` replaces its recorded under-counting line with the new behaviour, and the CHANGELOG entry states the change in what an existing consumer will record\n30\t- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`) and `npm run prettier:check` pass, with no `any` in the public API\n31\t\n32\t## Tech notes\n33\t\n34\tOut of scope: the audit entry or billing record itself, which is a consumer's concern; token accounting for anything other than the provider calls the orchestrator makes; and the `stop` chunk's own `usage` shape, which providers already populate and this issue only aggregates.\n35\t\n36\tKey files: `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` (the `lastUsage` local and the `afterModelResponse` call site), `src/types/ai.ts` (`UsageMetadata`), `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md`.\n37\t\n38\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"63a2195b-79a9-4aca-ae62-0168ef9ca155","uuid":"2f618b56-1577-4a21-9387-b9024b7bfe3e","timestamp":"2026-10-23T00:20:38.577Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/bb4499ef6246/workspace/ticket.md","content":"# Report usage across every tool round, not just the last\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#93\nLabels: priority:medium, type:feature, size:S, area:api\n\n## Why\n\n`afterModelResponse` is the hook a deployer reaches for when they need to record what a turn cost — an audit entry, a billing line, a usage budget. It is called once per user message, after the round loop exits, and is handed the `lastUsage` local: `chatOrchestrator.ts` assigns `lastUsage = outcome.usage ?? lastUsage` on each round, so what the hook receives is the usage of the final provider call alone.\n\nFor a turn that answered directly, that is the whole turn. For a turn that called tools, it is the last leg of several, and the earlier rounds — including the one carrying the customer's question and the full tool results — are not counted anywhere. A deployer billing or budgeting from this hook under-counts exactly the expensive turns, and nothing in the signature hints at it. The current behaviour is recorded in `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md` so it is at least discoverable, but documenting a surprising number is weaker than not producing one.\n\nRaised by the reviewer on HALEngine#83, which recorded the behaviour but could not change it: that PR's ticket scoped itself to specifying the seam, not altering the orchestrator.\n\n## What to do\n\nDecide, and make the signature say the answer. Two shapes are worth weighing, and the issue should record why the other was rejected:\n\n- Accumulate across rounds and pass the sum, keeping one `usage` argument. Simple for the common case, and it silently changes what an existing consumer records — the number gets larger for tool-heavy turns, which is the point, but it is a behaviour change worth a MINOR bump and a changelog line that says so.\n- Pass both, for example the summed total plus the per-round breakdown, so a consumer that wants provider-call granularity keeps it. More faithful, wider surface.\n\nWhichever is chosen, a turn that ran no tools must report exactly what it reports today, so the common path does not move.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] A turn driven through two tool rounds reports usage covering every provider call in that turn, asserted against a scripted provider whose rounds report known, different token counts\n- [ ] A turn with no tool calls reports exactly what it reports today, pinned by the existing test remaining unedited\n- [ ] A provider that reports no usage on some rounds does not make the total `undefined` or `NaN`: the rounds that reported are still counted, and a turn where nothing reported still reports nothing\n- [ ] The type of what the hook receives makes the answer unambiguous, so a reader does not have to open the orchestrator to learn whether it is a sum\n- [ ] `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md` replaces its recorded under-counting line with the new behaviour, and the CHANGELOG entry states the change in what an existing consumer will record\n- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`) and `npm run prettier:check` pass, with no `any` in the public API\n\n## Tech notes\n\nOut of scope: the audit entry or billing record itself, which is a consumer's concern; token accounting for anything other than the provider calls the orchestrator makes; and the `stop` chunk's own `usage` shape, which providers already populate and this issue only aggregates.\n\nKey files: `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` (the `lastUsage` local and the `afterModelResponse` call site), `src/types/ai.ts` (`UsageMetadata`), `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md`.\n\n","numLines":38,"startLine":1,"totalLines":38}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01NxocT69G4wQqgs5DaRE9DB","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

All three conditions matter; dropping the registry check spins the tool loop.\n\nconst A_TOOL: ToolCall[] = [{id: 't1', name: 'lookup', input: {}}];\nconst REGISTRY = {} as REGISTRY)).toBe(true);\n  });\n\n  it('stops when no registry is configured, whatever the model asked for', () => {\n    expect(shouldContinueToolLoop('tool_use', A_TOOL, undefined)).toBe(false);\n {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01QUZiSz9c5wjXyq1We8GnUH","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Config-Installed Orchestrator Hooks\n2\t\n3\t| Field  | Value                |\n4\t| ------ | -------------------- |\n5\t| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#36 |\n6\t| Status | Shipped              |\n7\t\n8\t`OrchestratorHooks` is the engine's only lifecycle seam, and `HalEngineConfig.orchestrator.hooks` is the supported way to install it: `createHalEngine` forwards the set to `createChatOrchestrator`, so a hook a deployer passes through the factory reaches the running orchestrator rather than sitting unread on the config object. This spec pins that forwarding and the measured semantics the seam already carries, so the hook-based features that follow in this epic — transparency disclosure, audit and usage recording, human oversight, provider failover — specify against real behaviour rather than against what the hook names suggest.\n9\t\n10\t## Installing hooks through config\n11\t\n12\t- A hook passed through `orchestrator.hooks` is forwarded to the orchestrator and fires ([validated by: forwards an orchestrator hook, so one passed through the config actually fires](../../src/config.test.ts#L19)).\n13\t- An engine built with no `orchestrator` key at all still processes a message, so the field is optional in fact and not only in the type ([validated by: processes a message when the config declares no orchestrator key at all](../../src/config.test.ts#L95)).\n14\t- A `beforeModelResponse` hook installed through config is handed the base system prompt built from `prompt`, proving the hook reaches the orchestrator and is wired into the prompt pipeline rather than only being stored on the config ([validated by: hands a config-installed beforeModelResponse hook the built base system prompt, not merely storing it](../../src/config.test.ts#L108)).\n15\t- The value the hook returns then replaces the system prompt the provider receives; this is exercised at the orchestrator layer because `HalEngineConfig.provider` takes a `ProviderConfig` and offers no seam to inject a recording provider through config ([validated by: replaces system prompt with returned value](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L221)).\n16\t- For a session that completes without error the hooks fire in the order `beforeSession` → `beforeUserInput` → `afterUserInput` → `beforeModelResponse` → `afterModelResponse` → `afterSession` ([validated by: fires the lifecycle hooks in documented order for a session that completes without error](../../src/config.test.ts#L132)).\n17\t- A `beforeSession` that throws reaches the caller as a rejection, and also runs `onError` with the thrown error and then `afterSession`, because the call sits inside the same `try`/`finally` as the rest of the turn. An `afterSession` hook called under this condition can infer the session never advanced past `beforeSession`: no user-input hooks ran, no model response was generated, and no tool rounds were started — a cleanup hook that allocates in `beforeSession` and frees in `afterSession` will therefore always see the allocation and the release on the same error path ([validated by: runs onError then afterSession when it throws, and still rejects to the caller](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L116)).\n18\t- `afterModelResponse` is called once per user message after the round loop exits and receives the `UsageMetadata` summed across every provider call in that turn, rather than only the final round's usage ([validated by: reports usage summed across every provider call when a turn runs through two tool rounds](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.acceptance.test.ts#L53)).\n19\t- When some rounds in a turn omit `usage`, `afterModelResponse` receives the sum of the rounds that reported it, and when no round in the turn reported `usage`, it receives `undefined` ([validated by: counts the rounds that reported usage when some omit it, and reports undefined when none did](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.acceptance.test.ts#L63)).\n20\t\n21\t## Engine startup\n22\t\n23\t- The engine's `start()` method rejects the returned promise when it cannot bind the configured port, rather than emitting an unhandled error event that would take the process down ([validated by: rejects instead of taking the process down with an unhandled error event](../../src/config.test.ts#L174)).\n24\t\n25\t## Measured semantics — out of scope\n26\t\n27\tThis property of `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` is recorded so the hook features that follow specify against real behaviour. This issue does not change it; it is a separate, out-of-scope change owned by no issue in this epic.\n28\t\n29\t- The `catch` gates `onError` on `error instanceof Error` ([chatOrchestrator.ts#L150](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts#L150)), so a rejection that is not an `Error` — a thrown string or object — bypasses `onError` entirely while still propagating to the caller.\n30\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"63a2195b-79a9-4aca-ae62-0168ef9ca155","uuid":"fa65b926-7460-4f9b-97f4-fdf4f02864dc","timestamp":"2026-10-23T00:20:46.869Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/bb4499ef6246/workspace/target/specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md","content":"# Config-Installed Orchestrator Hooks\n\n| Field  | Value                |\n| ------ | -------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#36 |\n| Status | Shipped              |\n\n`OrchestratorHooks` is the engine's only lifecycle seam, and `HalEngineConfig.orchestrator.hooks` is the supported way to install it: `createHalEngine` forwards the set to `createChatOrchestrator`, so a hook a deployer passes through the factory reaches the running orchestrator rather than sitting unread on the config object. This spec pins that forwarding and the measured semantics the seam already carries, so the hook-based features that follow in this epic — transparency disclosure, audit and usage recording, human oversight, provider failover — specify against real behaviour rather than against what the hook names suggest.\n\n## Installing hooks through config\n\n- A hook passed through `orchestrator.hooks` is forwarded to the orchestrator and fires ([validated by: forwards an orchestrator hook, so one passed through the config actually fires](../../src/config.test.ts#L19)).\n- An engine built with no `orchestrator` key at all still processes a message, so the field is optional in fact and not only in the type ([validated by: processes a message when the config declares no orchestrator key at all](../../src/config.test.ts#L95)).\n- A `beforeModelResponse` hook installed through config is handed the base system prompt built from `prompt`, proving the hook reaches the orchestrator and is wired into the prompt pipeline rather than only being stored on the config ([validated by: hands a config-installed beforeModelResponse hook the built base system prompt, not merely storing it](../../src/config.test.ts#L108)).\n- The value the hook returns then replaces the system prompt the provider receives; this is exercised at the orchestrator layer because `HalEngineConfig.provider` takes a `ProviderConfig` and offers no seam to inject a recording provider through config ([validated by: replaces system prompt with returned value](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L221)).\n- For a session that completes without error the hooks fire in the order `beforeSession` → `beforeUserInput` → `afterUserInput` → `beforeModelResponse` → `afterModelResponse` → `afterSession` ([validated by: fires the lifecycle hooks in documented order for a session that completes without error](../../src/config.test.ts#L132)).\n- A `beforeSession` that throws reaches the caller as a rejection, and also runs `onError` with the thrown error and then `afterSession`, because the call sits inside the same `try`/`finally` as the rest of the turn. An `afterSession` hook called under this condition can infer the session never advanced past `beforeSession`: no user-input hooks ran, no model response was generated, and no tool rounds were started — a cleanup hook that allocates in `beforeSession` and frees in `afterSession` will therefore always see the allocation and the release on the same error path ([validated by: runs onError then afterSession when it throws, and still rejects to the caller](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L116)).\n- `afterModelResponse` is called once per user message after the round loop exits and receives the `UsageMetadata` summed across every provider call in that turn, rather than only the final round's usage ([validated by: reports usage summed across every provider call when a turn runs through two tool rounds](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.acceptance.test.ts#L53)).\n- When some rounds in a turn omit `usage`, `afterModelResponse` receives the sum of the rounds that reported it, and when no round in the turn reported `usage`, it receives `undefined` ([validated by: counts the rounds that reported usage when some omit it, and reports undefined when none did](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.acceptance.test.ts#L63)).\n\n## Engine startup\n\n- The engine's `start()` method rejects the returned promise when it cannot bind the configured port, rather than emitting an unhandled error event that would take the process down ([validated by: rejects instead of taking the process down with an unhandled error event](../../src/config.test.ts#L174)).\n\n## Measured semantics — out of scope\n\nThis property of `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` is recorded so the hook features that follow specify against real behaviour. This issue does not change it; it is a separate, out-of-scope change owned by no issue in this epic.\n\n- The `catch` gates `onError` on `error instanceof Error` ([chatOrchestrator.ts#L150](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts#L150)), so a rejection that is not an `Error` — a thrown string or object — bypasses `onError` entirely while still propagating to the caller.\n","numLines":30,"startLine":1,"totalLines":30}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01QUZiSz9c5wjXyq1We8GnUH","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

type {Message, MessageChunk, ToolCall, UsageMetadata} from '../types/ai.js';\nimport type {ToolRegistry} from './tools/registry.js';\n\nexport function shouldContinueToolLoop(\n  stopReason: string,\n  pendingToolCalls: ToolCall[],\n  toolRegistry: ToolRegistry | undefined\n): boolean {\n  return stopReason === 'tool_use' && pendingToolCalls.length > 0 && toolRegistry !== undefined;\n}\n\nexport async function collectText(stream: AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk>): Promise<string> {\n  let text = '';\n  for await (const chunk of stream) {\n    if (chunk.type === 'text') text += chunk.text;\n  }\n  return text;\n}\n\nexport function buildToolCallMessages(pendingToolCalls: ToolCall[]): Message {\n  return {\n    role: 'assistant',\n    content: pendingToolCalls.map(tc => ({\n      type: 'tool_use' as const,\n      toolUseId: tc.id,\n      name: tc.name,\n      input: tc.input,\n    })),\n  };\n}\n\nexport function collectToolCalls(chunk: MessageChunk, pendingToolCalls: ToolCall[]): void {\n  if (chunk.type === 'tool_use') pendingToolCalls.push(chunk.toolCall);\n}\n\nexport function extractStopReason(chunk: MessageChunk): string | null {\n  return chunk.type === 'stop' ? chunk.stopReason : null;\n}\n\nexport function extractUsage(chunk: MessageChunk): UsageMetadata | undefined {\n  return chunk.type === 'stop' ? chunk.usage : undefined;\n}\n\nexport function accumulateUsage(\n  accumulated: UsageMetadata | undefined,\n  next: UsageMetadata | undefined\n): UsageMetadata | undefined {\n  if (accumulated === undefined) return next;\n  if (next === undefined) return accumulated;\n  return {\n    inputTokens: accumulated.inputTokens + next.inputTokens,\n    outputTokens: accumulated.outputTokens + next.outputTokens,\n    totalTokens: accumulated.totalTokens + next.totalTokens,\n  };\n}\n","numLines":56,"startLine":1,"totalLines":56}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01X5iW9aYFSV7qV79161aLH1","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

REGISTRY)).toBe(true);\n  });\n\n  it('stops when no registry is configured, whatever the model asked for', () => {\n    expect(shouldContinueToolLoop('tool_use', A_TOOL, undefined)).toBe(false);\n  });\n\n  it('stops when the model named no tool', () => {\n    expect(shouldContinueToolLoop('tool_use', [], REGISTRY)).toBe(false);\n  });\n\n  it('stops when the model finished for a reason other {createChatOrchestrator} from './chatOrchestrator.js';\nimport type {OrchestratorHooks} from './chatOrchestrator.js';\nimport type {AIProvider, MessageChunk, SendMessageParams, UsageMetadata} from '../types/ai.js';\nimport {AIError} from '../types/ai.js';\nimport type {ChatSession} from '../types/session.js';\nimport {PromptBuilder} from '../infrastructure/builders/promptBuilder.js';\nimport {ToolRegistry} from './tools/registry.js';\nimport type {ToolResponse} from './tools/registry.js';\nimport type {OutgoingMessage} from '../types/messages.js';\nimport type {SessionEntry} from '../types/session.js';\n\n// Fixed: nothing asserts on it, and new Date() would tie the run to the clock.\nconst SENT_AT = '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z';\n\n// One expect per test needs the rejection as a value rather than a matcher.\nconst outcomeOf = (call: Promise<unknown>): Promise<string> =>\n  call.then(() => 'resolved without throwing').catch((error: Error) => error.message);\n\nfunction createSession(content: string): ChatSession {\n  return {\n    sessionId: 's1',\n    userId: 'u1',\n    entries: [{role: 'user', content, timestamp: SENT_AT}],\n  };\n}\n\nfunction createMockProvider(response: string, usage?: UsageMetadata): AIProvider {\n  return {\n    async *sendMessage(_params: SendMessageParams): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n      yield {type: 'text', text: response};\n      yield {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'end_turn', usage};\n    },\n    async generateStructured<T>(): Promise<T> {\n      return {} as T;\n    },\n  };\n}\n\nfunction createErrorProvider(error: Error): AIProvider {\n  return {\n    // eslint-disable-next-line require-yield\n    async *sendMessage(): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n      throw error;\n    },\n    async generateStructured<T>(): Promise<T> {\n      return {} as T;\n    },\n  };\n}\n","numLines":50,"startLine":1,"totalLines":591}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01MnqB2aRXBvoTycJKGngPPX","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

undefined](../../src/orchestration/orchestratorHelpers.test.ts#L45)).","originalFile":"# Config-Installed Orchestrator Hooks\n\n| Field  | Value                |\n| ------ | -------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#36 |\n| Status | Shipped              |\n\n`OrchestratorHooks` is the engine's only lifecycle seam, and `HalEngineConfig.orchestrator.hooks` is the supported way to install it: `createHalEngine` forwards the set to `createChatOrchestrator`, so a hook a deployer passes through the factory reaches the running orchestrator rather than sitting unread on the config object. This spec pins that forwarding and the measured semantics the seam already carries, so the hook-based features that follow in this epic — transparency disclosure, audit and usage recording, human oversight, provider failover — specify against real behaviour rather than against what the hook names suggest.\n\n## Installing hooks through config\n\n- A hook passed through `orchestrator.hooks` is forwarded to the orchestrator and fires ([validated by: forwards an orchestrator hook, so one passed through the config actually fires](../../src/config.test.ts#L19)).\n- An engine built with no `orchestrator` key at all still processes a message, so the field is optional in fact and not only in the type ([validated by: processes a message when the config declares no orchestrator key at all](../../src/config.test.ts#L95)).\n- A `beforeModelResponse` hook installed through config is handed the base system prompt built from `prompt`, proving the hook reaches the orchestrator and is wired into the prompt pipeline rather than only being stored on the config ([validated by: hands a config-installed beforeModelResponse hook the built base system prompt, not merely storing it](../../src/config.test.ts#L108)).\n- The value the hook returns then replaces the system prompt the provider receives; this is exercised at the orchestrator layer because `HalEngineConfig.provider` takes a `ProviderConfig` and offers no seam to inject a recording provider through config ([validated by: replaces system prompt with returned value](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L221)).\n- For a session that completes without error the hooks fire in the order `beforeSession` → `beforeUserInput` → `afterUserInput` → `beforeModelResponse` → `afterModelResponse` → `afterSession` ([validated by: fires the lifecycle hooks in documented order for a session that completes without error](../../src/config.test.ts#L132)).\n- A `beforeSession` that throws reaches the caller as a rejection, and also runs `onError` with the thrown error and then `afterSession`, because the call sits inside the same `try`/`finally` as the rest of the turn. An `afterSession` hook called under this condition can infer the session never advanced past `beforeSession`: no user-input hooks ran, no model response was generated, and no tool rounds were started — a cleanup hook that allocates in `beforeSession` and frees in `afterSession` will therefore always see the allocation and the release on the same error path ([validated by: runs onError then afterSession when it throws, and still rejects to the caller](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L116)).\n- `afterModelResponse` is called once per user message after the round loop exits and receives the `UsageMetadata` summed across every provider call in that turn, rather than only the final round's usage ([validated by: reports usage summed across every provider call when a turn runs through two tool rounds](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.acceptance.test.ts#L53)).\n- When some rounds in a turn omit `usage`, `afterModelResponse` receives the sum of the rounds that reported it, and when no round in the turn reported `usage`, it receives `undefined` ([validated by: counts the rounds that reported usage when some omit it, and reports undefined when none did](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.acceptance.test.ts#L63)).\n\n## Engine startup\n\n- The engine's `start()` method rejects the returned promise when it cannot bind the configured port, rather than emitting an unhandled error event that would take the process down ([validated by: rejects instead of taking the process down with an unhandled error event](../../src/config.test.ts#L174)).\n\n## Measured semantics — out of scope\n\nThis property of `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` is recorded so the hook features that follow specify against real behaviour. This issue does not change it; it is a separate, out-of-scope change owned by no issue in this epic.\n\n- The `catch` gates `onError` on `error instanceof Error` ([chatOrchestrator.ts#L150](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts#L150)), so a rejection that is not an `Error` — a thrown string or object — bypasses `onError` entirely while still propagating to the caller.\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":15,"oldLines":8,"newStart":15,"newLines":8,"lines":[" - The value the hook returns then replaces the system prompt the provider receives; this is exercised at the orchestrator layer because `HalEngineConfig.provider` takes a `ProviderConfig` and offers no seam to inject a recording provider through config ([validated by: replaces system prompt with returned value](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L221))."," - For a session that completes without error the hooks fire in the order `beforeSession` → `beforeUserInput` → `afterUserInput` → `beforeModelResponse` → `afterModelResponse` → `afterSession` ([validated by: fires the lifecycle hooks in documented order for a session that completes without error](../../src/config.test.ts#L132))."," - A `beforeSession` that throws reaches the caller as a rejection, and also runs `onError` with the thrown error and then `afterSession`, because the call sits inside the same `try`/`finally` as the rest of the turn. An `afterSession` hook called under this condition can infer the session never advanced past `beforeSession`: no user-input hooks ran, no model response was generated, and no tool rounds were started — a cleanup hook that allocates in `beforeSession` and frees in `afterSession` will therefore always see the allocation and the release on the same error path ([validated by: runs onError then afterSession when it throws, and still rejects to the caller](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L116)).","-- `afterModelResponse` is called once per user message after the round loop exits and receives the `UsageMetadata` summed across every provider call in that turn, rather than only the final round's usage ([validated by: reports usage summed across every provider call when a turn runs through two tool rounds](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.acceptance.test.ts#L53)).","-- When some rounds in a turn omit `usage`, `afterModelResponse` receives the sum of the rounds that reported it, and when no round in the turn reported `usage`, it receives `undefined` ([validated by: counts the rounds that reported usage when some omit it, and reports undefined when none did](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.acceptance.test.ts#L63)).","+- `afterModelResponse` is called once per user message after the round loop exits and receives the `UsageMetadata` summed across every provider call in that turn, rather than only the final round's usage ([validated by: reports usage summed across every provider call when a turn runs through two tool {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Kt8Fw1H2Wos42sdLLTT9Lo","type":"tool_result","content":"15\t- The value the hook returns then replaces the system prompt the provider receives; this is exercised at the orchestrator layer because `HalEngineConfig.provider` takes a `ProviderConfig` and offers no seam to inject a recording provider through config ([validated by: replaces system prompt with returned value](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L221)).\n16\t- For a session that completes without error the hooks fire in the order `beforeSession` → `beforeUserInput` → `afterUserInput` → `beforeModelResponse` → `afterModelResponse` → `afterSession` ([validated by: fires the lifecycle hooks in documented order for a session that completes without error](../../src/config.test.ts#L132)).\n17\t- A `beforeSession` that throws reaches the caller as a rejection, and also runs `onError` with the thrown error and then `afterSession`, because the call sits inside the same `try`/`finally` as the rest of the turn. An `afterSession` hook called under this condition can infer the session never advanced past `beforeSession`: no user-input hooks ran, no model response was generated, and no tool rounds were started — a cleanup hook that allocates in `beforeSession` and frees in `afterSession` will therefore always see the allocation and the release on the same error path ([validated by: runs onError then afterSession when it throws, and still rejects to the caller](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L116)).\n18\t- `afterModelResponse` is called once per user message after the round loop exits and receives the `UsageMetadata` summed across every provider call in that turn, rather than only the final round's usage ([validated by: reports usage summed across every provider call when a turn runs through two tool rounds](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.acceptance.test.ts#L53)) ([validated by: sums token fields when both operands are defined](../../src/orchestration/orchestratorHelpers.test.ts#L29)).\n19\t- When some rounds in a turn omit `usage`, `afterModelResponse` receives the sum of the rounds that reported it, and when no round in the turn reported `usage`, it receives `undefined` ([validated by: counts the rounds that reported usage when some omit it, and reports undefined when none did](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.acceptance.test.ts#L63)) ([validated by: returns the next operand when the accumulated side is undefined](../../src/orchestration/orchestratorHelpers.test.ts#L35)) ([validated by: returns the accumulated operand when the next side is undefined](../../src/orchestration/orchestratorHelpers.test.ts#L40)) ([validated by: returns undefined when both operands are undefined](../../src/orchestration/orchestratorHelpers.test.ts#L45)).\n20\t\n21\t## Engine startup\n22\t\n23\t- The engine's `start()` method rejects the returned promise when it cannot bind the Left to find\n15\t// layers.yaml itself the rule would resolve every file as `src/...`, match no\n16\t// package, and report nothing - adopted in appearance, enforcing nothing. So\n17\t// the entries are re-homed under this checkout's own directory name with the\n18\t// root moved one level up, which is the shape the matcher expects.\n19\t//\n20\t// Paths come from this file's own URL rather than process.cwd(), so running\n21\t// eslint from a subdirectory cannot silently disable the rule.\n22\t// scripts/eslint-layers.test.ts fails if this ever stops reporting.\n23\tconst repoRoot = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));\n24\tconst declaredLayers = parseYaml(readFileSync(join(repoRoot, 'layers.yaml'), 'utf8')).layers.src;\n25\t\n26\t// Shared with eslint.config.backlog.mjs, which lints the same tree under the\n27\t// two rules held out of the blocking gate. One list, so the two configs cannot\n28\t// drift into disagreeing about what is even a source file.\n29\t//\n30\t// .claude/** is vendored agent tooling, maintained upstream rather than\n31\t// here, so a change there must not be able to redden this repo's CI.\n32\texport const ignores = [\n33\t  'node_modules/**',\n34\t  'dist/**',\n35\t  'coverage/**',\n36\t  '.claude/**',\n37\t  // Deliberately broken specs and ADRs - dead links, absent lead paragraphs,\n38\t  // statuses no parser reads - kept as the fixtures the script tests assert\n39\t  // against. Linting them would report the very faults they exist to reproduce.\n40\t  'scripts/fixtures/**',\n41\t];\n42\t\n43\texport default tseslint.config(\n44\t  {ignores},\n45\t  {\n46\t    // Scoped to TypeScript now that markdown is linted too: an unscoped block\n47\t    // applies to every language, and JS rules do not survive a markdown AST.\n48\t    files: ['**/*.ts'],\n49\t    extends: [eslint.configs.recommended, ...tseslint.configs.recommended],\n50\t    plugins: {'re-lint': reLint},\n51\t    rules: {\n52\t      // CLAUDE.md leads with five mandatory layers and \"no cross-layer\n53\t      // shortcuts, no circular deps\". layers.yaml states that as data and this\n54\t      // rule enforces it, so the architecture is a file someone can read rather\n55\t      // than a habit that erodes.\n56\t      're-lint/no-cross-layer-import': [\n57\t        'error',\n58\t        {root: dirname(repoRoot), layers: {[basename(repoRoot)]: declaredLayers}},\n59\t      ],\n60\t      '@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars': [\n61\t        'error',\n62\t        {\n63\t          vars: 'all',\n64\t          args: 'after-used',\n65\t          argsIgnorePattern: '^_',\n66\t          varsIgnorePattern: '^_',\n67\t          caughtErrorsIgnorePattern: '^_',\n68\t          ignoreRestSiblings: false,\n69\t        },\n70\t      ],\n71\t      camelcase: 'error',\n72\t      // CLAUDE.md: \"Tests live next to source: src/**/*.test.ts\". True of all\n73\t      // ten test files today; this keeps it true.\n74\t      're-lint/require-colocated-tests': 'error',\n75\t      're-lint/no-commented-out-code': 'error',\n76\t      // The provider layer must surface failures as AIError rather than\n77\t      // swallowing them and fabricating a return value (CLAUDE.md, Adding a\n78\t      // provider).\n79\t      're-lint/no-catch-as-control-flow': 'error',\n80\t      // Markdown is excluded because a doc quoting the code it documents is\n81\t      // the intended state: README's example of createHalEngine and the\n82\t      // interface blocks in specs/ are *supposed* to match src/. jscpd extracts\n83\t      // fenced blocks and reports them as clones, so without this every\n84\t      // accurate code sample is a lint error and the incentive is to let the\n85\t      // docs drift. The same exclusion a jscpd config would carry; it lives\n86\t      // here rather than in a .jscpd.json because the rule reads `ignore` from\n87\t      // its own options and never from a config file.\n88\t      're-lint/no-duplicate-code': ['error', {ignore: ['**/*.md']}],\n89\t      're-lint/callee-below-caller': 'error',\n90\t      're-lint/declare-near-use': 'error',\n91\t      're-lint/default-export-matches-filename': 'error',\n92\t      're-lint/max-boolean-operators': 'error',\n93\t      're-lint/max-comment-lines': 'error',\n94\t      're-lint/max-member-chain': 'error',\n95\t      're-lint/no-closing-brace-comments': 'error',\n96\t      're-lint/no-flag-params': 'error',\n97\t      // INERT, kept as a placeholder. It needs type information and this config\n98\t      // gives it none, so it reports nothing - verified with a textbook\n99\t      // forwarding class, which it does not flag. The rule's own docs record the\n100\t      // same finding for the same reason. It becomes a real guardrail only if\n101\t      // this config adopts parserOptions.projectService; until then it is not\n102\t      // one, and a clean run says nothing about forwarding classes.\n103\t      're-lint/no-forwarding-class': 'error',\n104\t      're-lint/no-hybrid-class': 'error',\n105\t      're-lint/no-negative-names': 'error',\n106\t      're-lint/no-nested-if': 'error',\n107\t      're-lint/no-nested-loop': 'error',\n108\t      're-lint/no-reexport-only-module': 'error',\n109\t      're-lint/no-vague-names': 'error',\n110\t      're-lint/prefer-early-return': 'error',\n111\t      're-lint/prefer-polymorphism': 'error',\n112\t      're-lint/require-fetch-timeout': 'error',\n113\t      'no-console': 'error',\n114\t      // Providers are loaded lazily so that selecting one never forces its SDK\n115\t      // on consumers of the others, and the factories doing it are synchronous\n116\t      // (`createProvider`, `createBedrockProvider`) - dynamic `import()` would\n117\t      // make them async, which is a breaking API change. The rule stays on and\n118\t      // still rejects every other `require()`; only these paths are permitted.\n119\t      '@typescript-eslint/no-require-imports': [\n120\t        'error',\n121\t        {allow: ['^\\\\./(bedrock|vertex|openai|anthropic|mock)/index\\\\.js$', '^@aws-sdk/client-bedrock-runtime$']},\n122\t      ],\n123\t    },\n124\t  },\n125\t  // Rules that only make sense on a test file.\n126\t  {\n127\t    // require-spec-link gates here: every test in\n128\t    // the suite cites a spec statement, so the backlog it once measured is\n129\t    // empty and an uncited new test is now a build failure rather than a\n130\t    // number. Its statement-side mirror stays a report - see\n131\t    // eslint.config.backlog.mjs.\n132\t    files: ['**/*.test.ts'],\n133\t    plugins: {'re-lint': reLint},\n134\t    rules: {\n135\t      're-lint/require-spec-link': 'error',\n136\t      're-lint/max-expects': 'error',\n137\t      're-lint/no-nondeterministic-tests': 'error',\n138\t      're-lint/test-imports-its-subject': 'error',\n139\t    },\n140\t  },\n141\t  // Every markdown link to a repo file must land. Left to find\n// layers.yaml itself the rule would resolve every file as `src/...`, match no\n// package, and report nothing - adopted in appearance, enforcing nothing. So\n// the entries are re-homed under this checkout's own directory name with the\n// root moved one level up, which is the shape the matcher expects.\n//\n// Paths come from this file's own URL rather than process.cwd(), so running\n// eslint from a subdirectory cannot silently disable the rule.\n// scripts/eslint-layers.test.ts fails if this ever stops reporting.\nconst repoRoot = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));\nconst declaredLayers = parseYaml(readFileSync(join(repoRoot, 'layers.yaml'), 'utf8')).layers.src;\n\n// Shared with eslint.config.backlog.mjs, which lints the same tree under the\n// two rules held out of the blocking gate. One list, so the two configs cannot\n// drift into disagreeing about what is even a source file.\n//\n// .claude/** is vendored agent tooling, maintained upstream rather than\n// here, so a change there must not be able to redden this repo's CI.\nexport const ignores = [\n  'node_modules/**',\n  'dist/**',\n  'coverage/**',\n  '.claude/**',\n  // Deliberately broken specs and ADRs - dead links, absent lead paragraphs,\n  // statuses no parser reads - kept as the fixtures the script tests assert\n  // against. Linting them would report the very faults they exist to reproduce.\n  'scripts/fixtures/**',\n];\n\nexport default tseslint.config(\n  {ignores},\n  {\n    // Scoped to TypeScript now that markdown is linted too: an unscoped block\n    // applies to every language, and JS rules do not survive a markdown AST.\n    files: ['**/*.ts'],\n    extends: [eslint.configs.recommended, ...tseslint.configs.recommended],\n    plugins: {'re-lint': reLint},\n    rules: {\n      // CLAUDE.md leads with five mandatory layers and \"no cross-layer\n      // shortcuts, no circular deps\". layers.yaml states that as data and this\n      // rule enforces it, so the architecture is a file someone can read rather\n      // than a habit that erodes.\n      're-lint/no-cross-layer-import': [\n        'error',\n        {root: dirname(repoRoot), layers: {[basename(repoRoot)]: declaredLayers}},\n      ],\n      '@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars': [\n        'error',\n        {\n          vars: 'all',\n          args: 'after-used',\n          argsIgnorePattern: '^_',\n          varsIgnorePattern: '^_',\n          caughtErrorsIgnorePattern: '^_',\n          ignoreRestSiblings: false,\n        },\n      ],\n      camelcase: 'error',\n      // CLAUDE.md: \"Tests live next to source: src/**/*.test.ts\". True of all\n      // ten test files today; this keeps it true.\n      're-lint/require-colocated-tests': 'error',\n      're-lint/no-commented-out-code': 'error',\n      // The provider layer must surface failures as AIError rather than\n      // swallowing them and fabricating a return value (CLAUDE.md, Adding a\n      // provider).\n      're-lint/no-catch-as-control-flow': 'error',\n      // Markdown is excluded because a doc quoting the code it documents is\n      // the intended state: README's example of createHalEngine and the\n      // interface blocks in specs/ are *supposed* to match src/. jscpd extracts\n      // fenced blocks and reports them as clones, so without this every\n      // accurate code sample is a lint error and the incentive is to let the\n      // docs drift. The same exclusion a jscpd config would carry; it lives\n      // here rather than in a .jscpd.json because the rule reads `ignore` from\n      // its own options and never from a config file.\n      're-lint/no-duplicate-code': ['error', {ignore: ['**/*.md']}],\n      're-lint/callee-below-caller': 'error',\n      're-lint/declare-near-use': 'error',\n      're-lint/default-export-matches-filename': 'error',\n      're-lint/max-boolean-operators': 'error',\n      're-lint/max-comment-lines': 'error',\n      're-lint/max-member-chain': 'error',\n      're-lint/no-closing-brace-comments': 'error',\n      're-lint/no-flag-params': 'error',\n      // INERT, kept as a placeholder. It needs type information and this config\n      // gives it none, so it reports nothing - verified with a textbook\n      // forwarding class, which it does not flag. The rule's own docs record the\n      // same finding for the same reason. It becomes a real guardrail only if\n      // this config adopts parserOptions.projectService; until then it is not\n      // one, and a clean run says nothing about forwarding classes.\n      're-lint/no-forwarding-class': 'error',\n      're-lint/no-hybrid-class': 'error',\n      're-lint/no-negative-names': 'error',\n      're-lint/no-nested-if': 'error',\n      're-lint/no-nested-loop': 'error',\n      're-lint/no-reexport-only-module': 'error',\n      're-lint/no-vague-names': 'error',\n      're-lint/prefer-early-return': 'error',\n      're-lint/prefer-polymorphism': 'error',\n      're-lint/require-fetch-timeout': 'error',\n      'no-console': 'error',\n      // Providers are loaded lazily so that selecting one never forces its SDK\n      // on consumers of the others, and the factories doing it are synchronous\n      // (`createProvider`, `createBedrockProvider`) - dynamic `import()` would\n      // make them async, which is a breaking API change. The rule stays on and\n      // still rejects every other `require()`; only these paths are permitted.\n      '@typescript-eslint/no-require-imports': [\n        'error',\n        {allow: ['^\\\\./(bedrock|vertex|openai|anthropic|mock)/index\\\\.js$', '^@aws-sdk/client-bedrock-runtime$']},\n      ],\n    },\n  },\n  // Rules that only make sense on a test file.\n  {\n    // require-spec-link gates here: every test in\n    // the suite cites a spec statement, so the backlog it once measured is\n    // empty and an uncited new test is now a build failure rather than a\n    // number. Its statement-side mirror stays a report - see\n    // eslint.config.backlog.mjs.\n    files: ['**/*.test.ts'],\n    plugins: {'re-lint': reLint},\n    rules: {\n      're-lint/require-spec-link': 'error',\n      're-lint/max-expects': 'error',\n      're-lint/no-nondeterministic-tests': 'error',\n      're-lint/test-imports-its-subject': 'error',\n    },\n  },\n  // Every markdown link to a repo file must land. A rename sweep rewrites a\n  // dead link faithfully and the reference still reads as current, which is\n  // exactly how the spec restructure could have gone wrong.\n  {\n    files: ['**/*.md'],\n    language: 'markdown/gfm',\n    plugins: {markdown, 're-lint': reLint},\n    rules: {'re-lint/no-dead-md-links': 'error'},\n  },\n  // AGENTS.md § Spec Header Table: every spec and ADR opens with a lead\n  // paragraph, and a spec's `| Status |` row must match its test-citation\n  // coverage. ADRs are exempt from the coverage tier - asking a decision\n  // record to cite a test for every statement it makes is the wrong ask - so\n  // the second rule is scoped to specs alone.\n  {\n    files: ['specs/**/spec.md', 'adrs/*.md', 'scripts/fixtures/spec-status/**/*.md'],\n    language: 'markdown/gfm',\n    plugins: {markdown, 're-lint': reLint},\n    rules: {'re-lint/require-intro-paragraph': 'error'},\n  },\n  {\n    files: ['specs/**/spec.md', 'scripts/fixtures/spec-status/specs/**/spec.md'],\n    language: 'markdown/gfm',\n    plugins: {markdown, 're-lint': reLint},\n    // require-statement-links belongs to this pair but is deliberately absent:\n    // see eslint.config.backlog.mjs.\n    rules: {'re-lint/require-status-matches-coverage': 'error'},\n  }\n);\n","numLines":170,"startLine":1,"totalLines":170}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01NDcoBhyinCcTH9iif1k4wF","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01JHR4PUWnFq8cxzXJqAXMTY","type":"tool_result","content":"diff --git a/specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md b/specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md\nindex 4a4b3db..e060efb 100644\n--- a/specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md\n+++ b/specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md\n@@ -15,8 +15,8 @@\n - The value the hook returns then replaces the system prompt the provider receives; this is exercised at the orchestrator layer because `HalEngineConfig.provider` takes a `ProviderConfig` and offers no seam to inject a recording provider through config ([validated by: replaces system prompt with returned value](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L221)).\n - For a session that completes without error the hooks fire in the order `beforeSession` → `beforeUserInput` → `afterUserInput` → `beforeModelResponse` → `afterModelResponse` → `afterSession` ([validated by: fires the lifecycle hooks in documented order for a session that completes without error](../../src/config.test.ts#L132)).\n - A `beforeSession` that throws reaches the caller as a rejection, and also runs `onError` with the thrown error and then `afterSession`, because the call sits inside the same `try`/`finally` as the rest of the turn. An `afterSession` hook called under this condition can infer the session never advanced past `beforeSession`: no user-input hooks ran, no model response was generated, and no tool rounds were started — a cleanup hook that allocates in `beforeSession` and frees in `afterSession` will therefore always see the allocation and the release on the same error path ([validated by: runs onError then afterSession when it throws, and still rejects to the caller](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L116)).\n-- `afterModelResponse` is called once per user message after the round loop exits and receives the `UsageMetadata` summed across every provider call in that turn, rather than only the final round's usage ([validated by: reports usage summed across every provider call when a turn runs through two tool rounds](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.acceptance.test.ts#L53)).\n-- When some rounds in a turn omit `usage`, `afterModelResponse` receives the sum of the rounds that reported it, and when no round in the turn reported `usage`, it receives `undefined` ([validated by: counts the rounds that reported usage when some omit it, and reports undefined when none {createChatOrchestrator} from './chatOrchestrator.js';\nimport type {OrchestratorHooks} from './chatOrchestrator.js';\nimport {ToolRegistry} from './tools/registry.js';\nimport {PromptBuilder} from '../infrastructure/builders/promptBuilder.js';\nimport type {AIProvider, MessageChunk, UsageMetadata} from '../types/ai.js';\nimport type {ChatSession} from '../types/session.js';\n\nconst reportedUsageFor = async (roundUsages: Array<UsageMetadata | undefined>): Promise<UsageMetadata | undefined> => {\n  let roundIndex = 0;\n  const provider: AIProvider = {\n    async *sendMessage(): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n      const currentRound = roundIndex++;\n      const usage = roundUsages[currentRound];\n      if (currentRound < roundUsages.length - 1) {\n        yield {type: 'tool_use', toolCall: {id: `call-${currentRound}`, name: 'lookup', input: {}}};\n        yield {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'tool_use', usage};\n        return;\n      }\n      yield {type: 'text', text: 'done'};\n      yield {type: 'stop', stopReason: 'end_turn', usage};\n    },\n    async generateStructured<T>(): Promise<T> {\n      return {} as T;\n    },\n  };\n\n  const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n  registry.register({name: 'lookup', description: 'Looks up an entry.', inputSchema: {type: 'object'}}, async () => ({\n    result: 'found',\n  }));\n\n  let reported: UsageMetadata | undefined;\n  const hooks: OrchestratorHooks = {\n    afterModelResponse: async (_session, _responseText, usage) => {\n      reported = usage;\n    },\n  };\n\n  const session: ChatSession = {\n    sessionId: 's-usage',\n    userId: 'u-usage',\n    entries: [{role: 'user', content: 'question', timestamp: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z'}],\n  };\n\n  const orchestrator = createChatOrchestrator(provider, new PromptBuilder({identity: 'Usage test.'}), registry, {\n    hooks,\n  });\n  await orchestrator.processMessage(session);\n  return reported;\n};\n\ndescribe('ChatOrchestrator multi-round usage reporting', () => {\n  it('reports usage summed across every provider call when a turn runs through two tool rounds', async () => {\n    const usage = await reportedUsageFor([\n      {inputTokens: 10, outputTokens: 4, totalTokens: 14},\n      {inputTokens: 25, outputTokens: 8, totalTokens: 33},\n      {inputTokens: 40, outputTokens: 12, totalTokens: 52},\n    ]);\n\n    expect(usage).toEqual({inputTokens: 75, outputTokens: 24, totalTokens: 99});\n  });\n\n  it('counts the rounds that reported usage when some omit it, and reports undefined when none did', async () => {\n    const someReported = await reportedUsageFor([\n      {inputTokens: 12, outputTokens: 5, totalTokens: 17},\n      undefined,\n      {inputTokens: 20, outputTokens: 7, totalTokens: 27},\n    ]);\n    const noneReported = await reportedUsageFor([undefined, undefined]);\n\n    expect({someReported, noneReported}).toEqual({\n      someReported: {inputTokens: 32, outputTokens: 12, totalTokens: 44},\n      noneReported: undefined,\n    });\n  });\n});\n","numLines":77,"startLine":1,"totalLines":77}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_012mvpGvKvFyhwFXSxmsGv7a","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Q6VJaD67uChhG1LWfGysbs","type":"tool_result","content":"## Installing hooks through config\n\n- A hook passed through `orchestrator.hooks` is forwarded to the orchestrator and fires ([validated by: forwards an orchestrator hook, so one passed through the config actually fires](../../src/config.test.ts#L19)).\n- An engine built with no `orchestrator` key at all still processes a message, so the field is optional in fact and not only in the type ([validated by: processes a message when the config declares no orchestrator key at all](../../src/config.test.ts#L95)).\n- A `beforeModelResponse` hook installed through config is handed the base system prompt built from `prompt`, proving the hook reaches the orchestrator and is wired into the prompt pipeline rather than only being stored on the config ([validated by: hands a config-installed beforeModelResponse hook the built base system prompt, not merely storing it](../../src/config.test.ts#L108)).\n- The value the hook returns then replaces the system prompt the provider receives; this is exercised at the orchestrator layer because `HalEngineConfig.provider` takes a `ProviderConfig` and offers no seam to inject a recording provider through config ([validated by: replaces system prompt with returned value](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L221)).\n- For a session that completes without error the hooks fire in the order `beforeSession` → `beforeUserInput` → `afterUserInput` → `beforeModelResponse` → `afterModelResponse` → `afterSession` ([validated by: fires the lifecycle hooks in documented order for a session that completes without error](../../src/config.test.ts#L132)).\n- A `beforeSession` that throws reaches the caller as a rejection, and also runs `onError` with the thrown error and then `afterSession`, because the call sits inside the same `try`/`finally` as the rest of the turn. An `afterSession` hook called under this condition can infer the session never advanced past `beforeSession`: no user-input hooks ran, no model response was generated, and no tool rounds were started — a cleanup hook that allocates in `beforeSession` and frees in `afterSession` will therefore always see the allocation and the release on the same error path ([validated by: runs onError then afterSession when it throws, and still rejects to the caller](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L116)).\n- `afterModelResponse` is called once per user message after the round loop exits and receives the `UsageMetadata` summed across every provider call in that turn, rather than only the final round's usage ([validated by: reports usage summed across every provider call when a turn runs through two tool rounds](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.acceptance.test.ts#L53)).\n- When some rounds in a turn omit `usage`, `afterModelResponse` receives the sum of the rounds that reported it, and when no round in the turn reported `usage`, it receives `undefined` ([validated by: counts the rounds that reported usage when some omit it, and reports undefined when none did](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.acceptance.test.ts#L63)).\n\n## Engine startup\n\n- The engine's `start()` method rejects the returned promise when it cannot bind the configured port, rather than emitting an unhandled error event that would take the process down ([validated by: rejects instead of taking the process down with an unhandled error event](../../src/config.test.ts#L174)).\n\n## Measured semantics — out of scope\n\nThis property of `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` is recorded so the hook features that follow specify against real behaviour. This issue does not change it; it is a separate, out-of-scope change owned by no issue in this epic.\n\n- The `catch` gates `onError` on `error instanceof Error` ([chatOrchestrator.ts#L150](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts#L150)), so a rejection that is not an `Error` — a thrown string or object — bypasses `onError` entirely while still propagating to the caller.","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"63a2195b-79a9-4aca-ae62-0168ef9ca155","uuid":"27b2ed27-10af-421d-b8ce-d4bc35bcac7d","timestamp":"2026-10-23T00:22:50.679Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"## Installing hooks through config\n\n- A hook passed through `orchestrator.hooks` is forwarded to the orchestrator and fires ([validated by: forwards an orchestrator hook, so one passed through the config actually fires](../../src/config.test.ts#L19)).\n- An engine built with no `orchestrator` key at all still processes a message, so the field is optional in fact and not only in the type ([validated by: processes a message when the config declares no orchestrator key at all](../../src/config.test.ts#L95)).\n- A `beforeModelResponse` hook installed through config is handed the base system prompt built from `prompt`, proving the hook reaches the orchestrator and is wired into the prompt pipeline rather than only being stored on the config ([validated by: hands a config-installed beforeModelResponse hook the built base system prompt, not merely storing it](../../src/config.test.ts#L108)).\n- The value the hook returns then replaces the system prompt the provider receives; this is exercised at the orchestrator layer because `HalEngineConfig.provider` takes a `ProviderConfig` and offers no seam to inject a recording provider through config ([validated by: replaces system prompt with returned value](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L221)).\n- For a session that completes without error the hooks fire in the order `beforeSession` → `beforeUserInput` → `afterUserInput` → `beforeModelResponse` → `afterModelResponse` → `afterSession` ([validated by: fires the lifecycle hooks in documented order for a session that completes without error](../../src/config.test.ts#L132)).\n- A `beforeSession` that throws reaches the caller as a rejection, and also runs `onError` with the thrown error and then `afterSession`, because the call sits inside the same `try`/`finally` as the rest of the turn. An `afterSession` hook called under this condition can infer the session never advanced past `beforeSession`: no user-input hooks ran, no model response was generated, and no tool rounds were started — a cleanup hook that allocates in `beforeSession` and frees in `afterSession` will therefore always see the allocation and the release on the same error path ([validated by: runs onError then afterSession when it throws, and still rejects to the caller](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L116)).\n- `afterModelResponse` is called once per user message after the round loop exits and receives the `UsageMetadata` summed across every provider call in that turn, rather than only the final round's usage ([validated by: reports usage summed across every provider call when a turn runs through two tool rounds](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.acceptance.test.ts#L53)).\n- When some rounds in a turn omit `usage`, `afterModelResponse` receives the sum of the rounds that reported it, and when no round in the turn reported `usage`, it receives `undefined` ([validated by: counts the rounds that reported usage when some omit it, and reports undefined when none did](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.acceptance.test.ts#L63)).\n\n## Engine startup\n\n- The engine's `start()` method rejects the returned promise when it cannot bind the configured port, rather than emitting an unhandled error event that would take the process down ([validated by: rejects instead of taking the process down with an unhandled error event](../../src/config.test.ts#L174)).\n\n## Measured semantics — out of scope\n\nThis property of `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` is recorded so the hook features that follow specify against real behaviour. This issue does not change it; it is a separate, out-of-scope change owned by no issue in this epic.\n\n- The `catch` gates `onError` on `error instanceof Error` ([chatOrchestrator.ts#L150](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts#L150)), so a rejection that is not an `Error` — a thrown string or object — bypasses `onError` entirely while still propagating to the caller.","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01Q6VJaD67uChhG1LWfGysbs","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012W6RSWZsoK5FnqGZv9UoUE","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/**\n2\t * require-spec-link — every `it()`/`test()` must be linked to a spec or ADR.\n3\t *\n4\t * The spec-traceability convention authors the link INSIDE the spec.md/adr `.md`\n5\t * as an inline trailing parenthetical — `Statement. ([validated by](test.ts#L42))`.\n6\t * This rule inverts that view: it goes over each test declaration and fails the\n7\t * ones that no spec or ADR statement references, so tests cannot silently drift\n8\t * out of spec coverage.\n9\t *\n10\t * A test is linked when the spec/adr corpus carries a `([validated by](this#Lnn))`\n11\t * link whose line falls inside the test's line span, or a whole-file link (no\n12\t * `#L`) to the file. The corpus is read once per process (memoized per specsRoot)\n13\t * via the shared parser — see `lib/spec-link-index.mjs`. Type info is off for\n14\t * test files, so this works from the ESTree AST + filename + filesystem only.\n15\t *\n16\t * Detect-only: writing the link needs a human to pick which statement the test\n17\t * validates, not a codemod.\n18\t */\n19\timport path from \"node:path\";\n20\timport { isTestFile } from \"./lib/spec-parsers.mjs\";\n21\timport { buildLinkIndex, corpusExists, readSpecFiles, toPosix, } from \"./lib/spec-link-index.mjs\";\n22\t/**\n23\t * specsRoot+roots → index, so the corpus is walked once per process.\n24\t *\n25\t * Note: the cache is keyed on specsRoot only, with no mtime/content hash — a\n26\t * single-pass `eslint`/CI run always reads fresh, but a long-lived ESLint\n27\t * server (VS Code extension, --watch) will not see a spec link added after the\n28\t * first lint until the server restarts.\n29\t */\n30\tconst indexCache = new Map();\n31\t/** specsRoots we've already warned about having no spec/adr corpus, so a\n32\t * misconfigured run warns once instead of once per linted test file.\n33\t *\n34\t * Scope is the whole Node process, matching `indexCache` above. In a one-shot\n35\t * `eslint`/CI run that is exactly one warning. In a long-lived ESLint server\n36\t * (VS Code, --watch) the module is not re-required between cycles, so once a\n37\t * specsRoot has warned it stays quiet even after the CWD is fixed — the same\n38\t * process-lifetime memoization caveat the corpus cache carries. Restart the\n39\t * server (or the editor) to re-arm the warning. */\n40\tconst warnedMissingCorpus = new Set();\n41\tfunction getIndex(specsRoot, roots) {\n42\t    // `\\0` separates every element so no root value can collide with the joiner.\n43\t    const key = [specsRoot, ...roots].join(\"\\0\");\n44\t    let index = indexCache.get(key);\n45\t    if (!index) {\n46\t        index = buildLinkIndex(readSpecFiles(specsRoot, roots));\n47\t        indexCache.set(key, index);\n48\t    }\n49\t    return index;\n50\t}\n51\t/** The base identifier of a (possibly chained) callee — `it` in `it.each([])(…)`. */\n52\tfunction rootCallName(callee) {\n53\t    let node = callee;\n54\t    while (node) {\n55\t        if (node.type === \"Identifier\") {\n56\t            return node.name;\n57\t        }\n58\t        if (node.type === \"MemberExpression\") {\n59\t            node = node.object;\n60\t            continue;\n61\t        }\n62\t        if (node.type === \"CallExpression\") {\n63\t            node = node.callee;\n64\t            continue;\n65\t        }\n66\t        // `it.each`table`(name, fn)` — the outer callee is a tagged template whose\n67\t        // `.tag` is the `it.each` member chain; drill into it or the test escapes.\n68\t        if (node.type === \"TaggedTemplateExpression\") {\n69\t            node = node.tag;\n70\t            continue;\n71\t        }\n72\t        return null;\n73\t    }\n74\t    return null;\n75\t}\n76\t/** A named test declaration: `it`/`test` (any modifier chain) with a string name. */\n77\tfunction isNamedTestCall(node) {\n78\t    const root = rootCallName(node.callee);\n79\t    if (root !== \"it\" && root !== \"test\") {\n80\t        return false;\n81\t    }\n82\t    const first = node.arguments[0];\n83\t    if (!first) {\n84\t        return false;\n85\t    }\n86\t    if (first.type === \"Literal\") {\n87\t        return typeof first.value === \"string\";\n88\t    }\n89\t    return first.type === \"TemplateLiteral\";\n90\t}\n91\tfunction isLinked(entry, startLine, endLine) {\n92\t    if (!entry) {\n93\t        return false;\n94\t    }\n95\t    if (entry.wholeFile) {\n96\t        return true;\n97\t    }\n98\t    for (const line of entry.lines) {\n99\t        if (line >= startLine && line <= endLine) {\n100\t            return true;"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"63a2195b-79a9-4aca-ae62-0168ef9ca155","uuid":"332e4fec-823f-48ec-8144-09d68dba81e3","timestamp":"2026-10-23T00:23:22.296Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/bb4499ef6246/workspace/target/node_modules/@re-cinq/eslint-plugin-re-lint/dist/rules/require-spec-link.mjs","content":"/**\n * require-spec-link — every `it()`/`test()` must be linked to a spec or ADR.\n *\n * The spec-traceability convention authors the link INSIDE the spec.md/adr `.md`\n * as an inline trailing parenthetical — `Statement. ([validated by](test.ts#L42))`.\n * This rule inverts that view: it goes over each test declaration and fails the\n * ones that no spec or ADR statement references, so tests cannot silently drift\n * out of spec coverage.\n *\n * A test is linked when the spec/adr corpus carries a `([validated by](this#Lnn))`\n * link whose line falls inside the test's line span, or a whole-file link (no\n * `#L`) to the file. The corpus is read once per process (memoized per specsRoot)\n * via the shared parser — see `lib/spec-link-index.mjs`. Type info is off for\n * test files, so this works from the ESTree AST + filename + filesystem only.\n *\n * Detect-only: writing the link needs a human to pick which statement the test\n * validates, not a codemod.\n */\nimport path from \"node:path\";\nimport { isTestFile } from \"./lib/spec-parsers.mjs\";\nimport { buildLinkIndex, corpusExists, readSpecFiles, toPosix, } from \"./lib/spec-link-index.mjs\";\n/**\n * specsRoot+roots → index, so the corpus is walked once per process.\n *\n * Note: the cache is keyed on specsRoot only, with no mtime/content hash — a\n * single-pass `eslint`/CI run always reads fresh, but a long-lived ESLint\n * server (VS Code extension, --watch) will not see a spec link added after the\n * first lint until the server restarts.\n */\nconst indexCache = new Map();\n/** specsRoots we've already warned about having no spec/adr corpus, so a\n * misconfigured run warns once instead of once per linted test file.\n *\n * Scope is the whole Node process, matching `indexCache` above. In a one-shot\n * `eslint`/CI run that is exactly one warning. In a long-lived ESLint server\n * (VS Code, --watch) the module is not re-required between cycles, so once a\n * specsRoot has warned it stays quiet even after the CWD is fixed — the same\n * process-lifetime memoization caveat the corpus cache carries. Restart the\n * server (or the editor) to re-arm the warning. */\nconst warnedMissingCorpus = new Set();\nfunction getIndex(specsRoot, roots) {\n    // `\\0` separates every element so no root value can collide with the joiner.\n    const key = [specsRoot, ...roots].join(\"\\0\");\n    let index = indexCache.get(key);\n    if (!index) {\n        index = buildLinkIndex(readSpecFiles(specsRoot, roots));\n        indexCache.set(key, index);\n    }\n    return index;\n}\n/** The base identifier of a (possibly chained) callee — `it` in `it.each([])(…)`. */\nfunction rootCallName(callee) {\n    let node = callee;\n    while (node) {\n        if (node.type === \"Identifier\") {\n            return node.name;\n        }\n        if (node.type === \"MemberExpression\") {\n            node = node.object;\n            continue;\n        }\n        if (node.type === \"CallExpression\") {\n            node = node.callee;\n            continue;\n        }\n        // `it.each`table`(name, fn)` — the outer callee is a tagged template whose\n        // `.tag` is the `it.each` member chain; drill into it or the test escapes.\n        if (node.type === \"TaggedTemplateExpression\") {\n            node = node.tag;\n            continue;\n        }\n        return null;\n    }\n    return null;\n}\n/** A named test declaration: `it`/`test` (any modifier chain) with a string name. */\nfunction isNamedTestCall(node) {\n    const root = rootCallName(node.callee);\n    if (root !== \"it\" && root !== \"test\") {\n        return false;\n    }\n    const first = node.arguments[0];\n    if (!first) {\n        return false;\n    }\n    if (first.type === \"Literal\") {\n        return typeof first.value === \"string\";\n    }\n    return first.type === \"TemplateLiteral\";\n}\nfunction isLinked(entry, startLine, endLine) {\n    if (!entry) {\n        return false;\n    }\n    if (entry.wholeFile) {\n        return true;\n    }\n    for (const line of entry.lines) {\n        if (line >= startLine && line <= endLine) {\n            return true;","numLines":100,"startLine":1,"totalLines":186}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_012W6RSWZsoK5FnqGZv9UoUE","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

Warn once and skip rather than\n108\t// report thousands of misleading errors.\n109\tfunction warnMissingCorpusOnce(specsRoot, roots) {\n110\t    if (warnedMissingCorpus.has(specsRoot)) {\n111\t        return;\n112\t    }\n113\t    warnedMissingCorpus.add(specsRoot);\n114\t    console.warn(`require-spec-link: no ${roots.join(\"/\")} directory under ${specsRoot} — ` +\n115\t        \"run eslint from the repo root (or pass options.specsRoot). Warn once and skip rather than\n// report thousands of misleading errors.\nfunction warnMissingCorpusOnce(specsRoot, roots) {\n    if (warnedMissingCorpus.has(specsRoot)) {\n        return;\n    }\n    warnedMissingCorpus.add(specsRoot);\n    console.warn(`require-spec-link: no ${roots.join(\"/\")} directory under ${specsRoot} — ` +\n        \"run eslint from the repo root (or pass options.specsRoot). Skipping the spec-link check.\");\n}\nfunction testName(sourceCode, first) {\n    return first.type === \"Literal\"\n        ? String(first.value)\n        : sourceCode.getText(first);\n}\nfunction reportUnlinked(context, node, corpus) {\n    if (!isNamedTestCall(node)) {\n        return;\n    }\n    const startLine = node.loc.start.line;\n    if (isLinked(corpus.entry, startLine, node.loc.end.line)) {\n        return;\n    }\n    context.report({\n        node: node.callee,\n        messageId: \"unlinkedTest\",\n        data: {\n            name: testName(context.sourceCode, node.arguments[0]),\n            file: corpus.relPath,\n            line: String(startLine),\n            roots: corpus.roots.map((root) => `${root}/**/*.md`).join(\" or \"),\n        },\n    });\n}\nexport default {\n    meta: {\n        type: \"problem\",\n        docs: {\n            description: \"require every test to be linked from a spec or ADR via an inline ([validated by](test.ts#Lline)) link.
